| 128 | 1.0.10-beta · (the maintainer, 2026-09-25) | Overtaken by 553 (the later row in force, 730); the text as decided: **One context wrapper: `@Component<C, T>`** **Decided 2026-09-25 by the maintainer**, amending the effect revision before it lands: *"gostaria de unificar `@Use<C, T>` or `@Component<T>` em `@Component<C, T>`"*. The two wrappers that grant `use` become one: [Return · The body may write — `@Component<C, T>` · `use` · `await`] (1) **`C` is the base** the body's `use`s anchor at (`ElementBase`, `RequestBase` — the library's name; the compiler knows none). It is **always written**, for a hook and for a component; it is never read off `T`'s `implement @Context<…>` clause. `@Component<T>` with one argument is a type-arity error. (2) **`T` is the return.** A **hook** returns any `T` (`@Component<ElementBase, State<T>>`); a **component** is the `@Component<C, T>` whose `T` implements `@Context<C>` (`@Component<ElementBase, Element>`). The rules of 96 / 104 stand, read off this one wrapper: one base per function; hooks compose (`use h()` needs `h: @Component<C, _>` with the body's `C`); a component is called (`Counter()`), never `use`d — `use` of a call whose `T` owns the context is refused. (3) **The chain** is `@Component ⊃ @Task` (`@Stream ⊃ @Task`, `@Iterator`); `throw` / `try` follow 121 (legal when `T` is a `@Result`); commonJS lowers every `@Component` return to an `async function` (120). (4) **`@Use` leaves** without an alias (127): `@Use<C, T>` in a type is `effect-type-removed`, fix-it `@Component<C, T>`. The codemod rewrites `@Use<C, T>` → `@Component<C, T>` and `@Component<T>` → `@Component<B, T>`, `B` read from `T implement @Context<B>` (a typed rewrite, like `try await`). (5) `effect-wrapper-mismatch`'s last case — `@Component<X>` with `X` owning no context — leaves with the sugar: any `T` is a legal hook return. `pub fn state<T>(initial: T) -> @Component<ElementBase, State<T>> { … } // a hook · pub fn counter(start: i32) -> @Component<ElementBase, #(i32, fn() -> void)> { · val s = use state(start); · return #(s.get(), fn() { s.set(s.get() + 1); }); · } · #[page] · pub fn PostPage(ctx: PageContext) -> @Component<ElementBase, Element> { // a component · val (n, inc) = use counter(0); · return div([p([text(n.toString())])]); · }` **Amends:** (1) **102** — `@Use<C, T>` and `@Component<T>` ≡ `@Use<B, T>` become `@Component<C, T>`; the sugar that read `B` from `T` leaves. `@Context<Base>` as the owner marker only stays. (2) **104** — its rules are read off `@Component<C, T>`; "the base of a `@Component<T>` is the `B` of `T: @Context<B>`" becomes "the base is `C`". (3) **117 item 3** — `#[layout]` / `#[page]` / `#[template]` require `-> @Component<ElementBase, Element>`. (4) **118** — the table's `@Use<C, T>` or `@Component<T>` row is `@Component<C, T>`; rules 3 and 6 name one wrapper. (5) **120, 121** — the chain is `@Component ⊃ @Task`; the await channel and the commonJS `async function` key on `@Task` / `@Component`; `@Component<C, @Result<…>>` carries a hook's failure. Implements: front [`24-effects-by-return`](1.0.10-beta/00-compiler-carry-over/24-effects-by-return/README.md) steps E1 (`Component<C, T> extends Task`, `Use` removed), E2 (`@Use` → `effect-type-removed`), E3 (the `use` gate and the component-is-called refusal keyed on `T: @Context<C>`), E6 (the two codemod rows) and E8 (the spec examples). | front `24-effects-by-return` steps E1 (`Component<C, T> extends Task`, `Use` removed), E2 (`@Use` → `effect-type-removed`), E3 (the `use` gate and the component-is-called refusal keyed on `T: @Context<C>`), E6 (the two codemod rows) and E8 (the spec examples) | |

| 136 | 0203-a · (the maintainer, 2026-09-26) | **A primitive receiver answers only the methods it declares** **Decided 2026-09-26 by the maintainer**, answering pending 0203-a with option (b). A method call on a builtin-primitive receiver (`string`, `T[]`, `bool`, the integer and float widths) is legal only when the receiver's interface in `primitives.bp` — or one it `extends` — declares it: a `fn` method, a `val` field, the `len` / `size` spellings of `length`, or a method an `extend` block on the type adds. Anything else is refused on every target: (1) **`unknown-primitive-method`**, located at the method name: `` `string` has no method `fooBar` ``. (2) **A near name is suggested.** The declared method whose `#[@External.Node("<name>")]` host spelling is the name written comes first (`"abc".toUpperCase()` → *did you mean `toUpper`?*), then one an edit away (`xs.lenght()` → `length`). (3) **A host spelling is not a method.** `#[@External.Node("toUpperCase")]` says how commonJS lowers `toUpper`; it adds no name to the language. The backend aliases that answered the host spelling leave: erlang / beam's `primNodeAliasIn` and its two call sites (0203-a (a)), and wasm's `toUpperCase` / `toLowerCase` rows of `primCallRes` and `lowerStringMethod`. It used to type as a fresh variable and reach the host under the name written: `"x".fooBar()` checked, commonJS answered `toUpperCase` because it is JavaScript's own, wasm answered both spellings and erlang emitted `toUpperCase/1 undefined` — three answers for one program. Decision 67: refuse. Implements: front [`01-checker`](1.0.10-beta/00-compiler-carry-over/01-checker/README.md), compiler `9dfeedf6` (`refuseUndeclaredPrimMethod` in `comptime/infer.zig`; `reject/primitive_method_undeclared`, `reject/primitive_method_unknown`; `test/string_case_conversion` re-spelled `toUpper` / `toLower`). `primNodeAliasIn` exists only on `feat` after this front's base; it is deleted when the front merges `feat`. | front `01-checker`, compiler `9dfeedf6` (`refuseUndeclaredPrimMethod` in `comptime/infer.zig`; `reject/primitive_method_undeclared`, `reject/primitive_method_unknown`; `test/string_case_conversion` re-spelled `toUpper` / `toLower`). `primNodeAliasIn` exists only on `feat` after this front's base; it is deleted when the front merges `feat` |  primitive_receiver_unknown_method_refused |
| 138 | 1.0.10-beta · (the maintainer, 2026-09-26) | **The empty record is `type X()`; the brace-only form is refused** **Decided 2026-09-26 by the maintainer**: *"the correct is `pub type RequestBase();` or `pub type RequestBase() {};`"*. A record always writes its field list, so the shape of a `type` is read off what was written — a field list (`()` included) is a record, braces holding a variant or a section are an enum: `pub type RequestBase() // the empty record · type MathOps() { // an empty record with members · fn double(self: Self, x: i32) -> i32 { return x * 2; } · }` The empty field list `()` stops being `type-empty-field-list`. A `type` that ends with neither a field list nor a variant — `type X {}`, `type X { fn … }`, a bare `type X`, and the val-form `type {}` — is `type-without-field-list`, located where the `()` belongs (after the name and generics), with the fix-it `type Name()`. One spelling (decision 67). `botopink format` prints `()` for every record with no fields, and an LSP hover card does the same. **Amends:** decision 12's shape resolution (front 12's `type-grammar.md`: "`type Name { methods }` / `type Name` → record with no fields"). Implements: `parser/decls.zig` (`parseTypeDeclRest`, `parseFieldList`), `format.zig`, `language-server/src/engine.zig`; every record in the compiler repository re-spelled (std's bundled `validation` test, the `tests/language` cells, the unit-test sources); cells `run/type_empty_record`, `reject/{type_empty_braces,type_without_field_list}`; the guide's § 4.1 / § 4.4 (`pub type ElementBase();`, `pub type RequestBase();`). The libraries re-spell their own — the list is in `status.md`. | `parser/decls.zig` (`parseTypeDeclRest`, `parseFieldList`), `format.zig`, `language-server/src/engine.zig`; every record in the compiler repository re-spelled (std's bundled `validation` test, the `tests/language` cells, the unit-test sources); cells `run/type_empty_record`, `reject/{type_empty_braces,type_without_field_list}`; the guide's § 4.1 / § 4.4 (`pub type ElementBase();`, `pub type RequestBase();`) |  empty_record_written_with_parens |
| 139 | 0405-a · (the maintainer, 2026-09-26) | **A negative index counts from the end, on every backend** **Decided 2026-09-26 by the maintainer** (pending item 0405-a, reversed): a negative position counts from the end, the way native `Array.prototype.at` already did on commonJS — and now on all four backends, so no program reads "the last element" on one and "absent" on another. `` val xs = [1, 2, 3]; · xs.at(-1); // 3 · xs.at(-3); // 1 · xs.at(-4); // null — past the front · xs.at(3); // null — past the back · xs[-1]; // 3 — an index is `.at` (decision 63) · "abc".at(-1); // "c" `` The rule is `Array.at` and `String.at` — and therefore `xs[i]` and `s[i]`, which the checker rewrites to them. The answer stays decision 47's `?T`: absent past either end. `Dict.at` is by key and has no position to count from; a user type's `at` (decision 63's `Index<K, V>`) decides for itself. A tuple's constant index is the checker's own case and is unaffected. Per backend: commonJS `__bp_array_at` / `__bp_string_char_at` are native `.at(i) ?? null`; erlang `primitives.bp`'s templates add the length to a negative index (beam evaluates the `String.at` one); beam's `'-bp_at-'/2` branches on the sign; wasm's `$__arr_at`, `$__arr_at_box` and `$__str_at` add the length before their bounds test. **Amends:** `00 · 04-js`'s C-18 half (0405-a). Implements: `codegen/js/js_prelude.zig`, `libs/std/src/primitives.bp`, `codegen/beam_asm.zig`, `codegen/wat/wat_prelude.zig`, `codegen/erlang.zig`'s run-time `'__bp_index'/2`; cell `tests/language/run/index_negative_from_end`; the codegen snapshots that carry the helpers re-recorded in both trees (the RUN LOGs unchanged). | `codegen/js/js_prelude.zig`, `libs/std/src/primitives.bp`, `codegen/beam_asm.zig`, `codegen/wat/wat_prelude.zig`, `codegen/erlang.zig`'s run-time `'__bp_index'/2`; cell `tests/language/run/index_negative_from_end`; the codegen snapshots that carry the helpers re-recorded in both trees (the RUN LOGs unchanged) |  negative_index_counts_from_end |
| 142 | 01std-b · (the maintainer, 2026-09-26) | **`json.decode` converts a numeral exactly, in botopink** **Decided 2026-09-26 by the maintainer** (pending item 01std-b): option (b), without its limit — the conversion is botopink's own and correctly rounded for every numeral, not only in the fast-path range. `libs/std/src/json.bp`'s `numeralValue` reads the validated numeral's digits and exponent from the document and answers the `f64` nearest to its exact decimal value, ties to even: (1) Clinger's fast path when it is exact — at most 15 significant digits and a decimal exponent in -22…22: the digits and the power of ten are exact `f64`s, so one operation rounds once; (2) otherwise the exact fraction of big integers (15-bit limbs in `Array<i32>`, every product below 2^31 on every target): the binary exponent chosen for a 53-bit quotient (the subnormal grid below 2^-1022), the quotient found by shift-and-subtract, the remainder rounding it; (3) the `f64` assembled with no integer → float conversion — the quotient's bits accumulate into an `f64`, exact power-of-two multiplications scale it — so no primitive was missing and no host cell remains for it. An overflow keeps the refusal (`number overflows f64`); a value below half the least subnormal is `0.0`. Measured: bit-identical to the host `strtod` for every tested value — the std test's boundaries (`1.7976931348623157e308` and the halfway point above it, `5e-324` and the halfway point below it, `2.2250738585072014e-308` and the largest subnormal, `0.1`, `1e23`, `9007199254740993`, the fast path's edges, overflowing and vanishing exponents) and 3 500 randomized numerals including halfway points, on commonJS and erlang. Implements: `libs/std/src/json.bp` and its test, `libs/std/AGENTS.md`. | `libs/std/src/json.bp` and its test, `libs/std/AGENTS.md` |  json_decode_numeral_correctly_rounded |

| 354 | 134-f · (contexts) | **A component is `@Component<R>`; what a body reads from above it is a context, provided by a hook — React's context in botopink's spelling.** (1) **The wrapper:** `@Component<R>`; 128's base parameter goes (`@Component<C, R>` is a type-arity error naming `@Component<R>`; the codemod drops `C`). `@Component` is the only wrapper — no `@Hook`: a `@Component<R>` whose `R` implements `@Renderable` (`Element implement @Renderable`; the marker `@Context<C>` goes) is called as a tag or a function, and any other `@Component<R>` is read with `use` (a hook); `use` and `await` in the body as before (128's chain stands). (2) **A context** always declares its default, named by its value's type: `pub val themeContext = comptime createContext(Theme(mode: .Light));` (std's `context` module; one declaration per type; 378, 379). **Provide:** `use provide(createContext(Theme(mode: .Dark)));` gives the value to everything that component renders below it — descendants only, never itself or a sibling; the nearest provider wins. **Read:** `val t = use context(Theme);` → `Theme`; with no provider above, the declared default. (3) **Only where there is a render tree:** a render at the comptime stage (a prerendered page, 186, 202), the server, the client. A decorator body, a template body and a `comptime { … }` have no tree: `use` there is refused, and what they need comes from the catalogue (353) — the app's `#[theme]` stays a catalogue entry, not a context; a `Build` / `Any` component may be called at build (378 (4)). (4) **Checked at build:** `Decl.hooks` (277) lists each `provide` / `context` with its object; a framework's root decorator (`#[page]`, rakun's entry) checks that every context read has a provider on its path, the root's included — an error at the `use` naming the context; `context-getcontext-anchor-violation` goes. (5) **Stage:** a context declared `#[serverOnly]` / `#[clientOnly]` (186) restricts its readers — `use context(Request)` with `#[serverOnly] pub val RequestContext = createContext(…);` makes a page `D`; an unmarked context is readable at any stage. (6) **The island seam:** a `#[client]` island (or a `#[serverDefer]` one) reading a context provided outside it receives the value in its payload when `T` is encodable (`#[validated]`, or a type 294 accepts), sealed for a server island (272); otherwise the island provides it inside, or the island's `use` is an error (cardume's `atm-c` stays its own question). (7) **The frameworks' bases become root contexts:** jhonstart's render provides `ElementContext`, rakun's pipeline `RequestContext`, jhonstart's style integration and emilia's `flush()` `StyledContext` (352); at a prerender `StyledContext` holds the build's sheet (124). (8) **Lowering:** 388 (a component is a lambda over a `RenderScope`). | 388 | 134-g · (render) | **A `@Component<R>` at run time is a lambda over an opaque `RenderScope`; calling a component only makes the lambda, and the render library decides who runs it, when, and with which scope.** (1) `fn Card(item: Item) -> View` lowers to a function answering `(scope) => …`: the call `Card(i)` runs nothing, so `itens.map(Card)` answers lambdas and generic code needs nothing of a component. (2) std's `context` module declares `RenderScope` — opaque, made only by `RenderScope.root()` — and a component value's `c.run(scope)`, answering the result and the scope its body's `provide`s made for its children (the record's spelling is the implementing front's; a `@Task` when 375 marks the node asynchronous). (3) The render library — jhonstart's renderer — holds scopes explicitly: it runs the root with `RenderScope.root()`, each child with the children's scope its parent answered, and a value it keeps for later inside a host's try or a `Suspense` fill with the scope it kept (414). (4) In a body, `use provide(ctx)` makes the children's scope; `use context(T)` reads the scope the lambda received, else the declared default (378, 379). Inside a component body `await c` runs `c` with the body's children scope; outside every body (a module `val`, a test) a value runs with `c.run(RenderScope.root())`; at build (378 (4)) the comptime runtime runs it with `RenderScope.root()`. (5) The scope travels explicitly, so two renders never share one on any target — no "current context" stack, no `AsyncLocalStorage`, no process dictionary; nothing is captured. (6) The rewrite of the built lowering (`comptime/context_lower.zig`) waits on `01-compiler/134` step 6's measurement; until it lands a component value handed to generic code stays refused at build (`component-value-to-generic`, naming the template's `for`). | 592 | B-55-a · (the maintainer, 2026-10-11) | **(e), a counter-proposal: a file is a module, and the build has a layer of its own.** (1) Every `.bp` under `src/` is a module of the package, its path its name (`pages.home`, `comptime.route`); no `lib.bp`, no `mod` / `pub mod`. (2) `src/main.bp` is an executable's entry (`pub fn main()`), imported by no module; a library has none. (3) `src/comptime/` is the build layer: analysed before every other module of the package, it imports only std, dependencies and `src/comptime/` itself (else refused, located); a decorator or a template is declared only there, in std or in a dependency (else refused, located). (4) The program's modules import each other freely: modules importing each other form a block (a strongly connected component); a block's headers (`fn` signatures, types, annotated `val`s) are registered before its bodies; at run time a module-level `val` is a ref, a memoised getter (commonJS hoists its functions onto `exports` before any `require`). (5) Per block: headers, bodies, then every decorator over them (the build layer is already compiled), then every `@typeInfo(X).meta(T)` and catalogue of the block is filled — 130-s8-c's step 4, per block. (6) The only order refusal is `dependency loop`, located: a value needing itself across modules (`pub val x = b.y + 1` / `pub val y = a.x + 1`), or a `comptime` needing a meta, a catalogue or a value of its own block before its decorators ran. (7) the catalogue is `compiler.find`, read at build only (576 (7), 581 (3); `@TypeInfo.all` is gone). (8) `src/internal/` is private to its package; every other `pub` item is the package's API. (9) The prelude (116-a) is any marked `pub` item outside `internal/`. Replaces s23-i's root rules (`lib.bp`, `mod`, the catalogue in the root), 419's and 14s8-d's refusals and 353's second compile; B-56 stands. The cost on `zig build test` and on the language server is measured before the implementation steps; a high cost returns the question to the maintainer. | `1-botopink-lang/144` B-55, B-69 … B-76 · 130-s8-c step 4 · s23-i | file_is_module_build_layer |
| 595 | 537-a · (the maintainer, 2026-10-17) | **`try` opens a region; `catch` closes it and handles the failure of every `try` written in it.** (1) A `try` propagates its failure to the nearest region holding it: a `try { … } catch …`, else the function (which then needs a `@Result` channel). (2) The region is the block `try { … }` when written, else the whole expression left of the `catch` (537 (2)): `try e catch h` is `try { e } catch h`, and `(try a) + (try b) catch 0` sends both failures to the one `catch`. (3) The handler is one of: an expression (the fallback, `catch 0`); a `throw` (`catch throw ParseFailed` — the failure becomes the function's); a block whose value is the fallback (`catch { log.warn("…"); 0 }`); a block with a binder, `catch { e -> … }`, `e` the region's error — a `case` arm's binder, not a lambda, typed by the region. (4) The region is an expression: its value is its last expression written without `;` (409, 525), the handler produces the same type; an empty handler `catch { }` is refused `catch-empty`, located — a failure is handled (a value, a statement, a `throw`) or left to propagate, never swallowed in silence (67); a `void` region's handler is a block with at least one statement (`try { try save(a); try save(b) } catch { log.warn("not saved") }`). (5) One `E` per region: every `try` in a region fails with the same error type; a second type is refused at that `try`, located, naming both — `try-region-errors-differ`: `read` fails with `IoError`, `parseInt` with `ParseError` — handle one on the spot (`try parseInt(raw) catch …`) or convert it (`catch throw …`). (6) A `try` in the handler's body belongs to the enclosing region or the function, never to its own `catch`. (7) A region holding no `try` is refused `try-region-cannot-fail`, located. (8) Unchanged: `defer` (539) runs when its block ends, a caught failure ending the region and the inner blocks on the way; `attempt` (431, 444) alone catches `panic` / `crash`, `catch` only a `@Result`'s failure; 537 (1), (2) — `try` and `await` prefixes, `catch` the lowest infix (537-b stays open). (9) Lowering: one early-exit block per region on each target — commonJS a labelled block or a sentinel, erlang / beam `try … catch` over a term of its own, wasm `block … br` to the handler; no new exception on wasm (518 untouched). Cells `try_block_catch_value`, `try_block_catch_binder`, `try_block_catch_rethrow`, `try_block_catch_void_statement`, `try_block_catch_empty` (refused), `catch_region_covers_left_side`, `try_in_catch_body_propagates`, the refusals `try_region_errors_differ`, `try_region_cannot_fail`; `conformance/docs.md` § @Result. Every form written today stays valid. Amends 537. | `1-botopink-lang/144` (machine 2: parser, checker, four backends, formatter) · `conformance/docs.md` | try_region_closed_by_catch |

A decorator adds and never merges or replaces (404). Module-level `@emit` goes (373). (0) Every parameter of a decorator is `comptime x: @Expr<T>` (364), written explicitly (a parameter without it is an error at the decorator's declaration); its arguments exist only while the program is compiled — what the program uses at run time comes from the decorator's outputs (216); its value is read with `x.value` when known at build, and an argument not known at build is refused only where `.value` reads it; a function or a type is passed on, never called or inspected. (1) An argument may be of any type — a function, a `type`, a `compiler.Field<T>` (308), an enum, a record — checked at the argument. (2) `@Decl<T>`: `T` is the annotated declaration's type (a type, a field's type, a function's type), inferred when the decorator's signature uses `T` in another parameter, also through a pattern (`@Decl<fn(e: E) -> unknown>` binds `E`); with no use it is `unknown`, and `@Decl` is `@Decl<unknown>`. (3) `compiler.Field<T>` (308): a typed reference to a field of `T` carrying its name; the shorthand `.name` resolves against the expected `T`, a missing field an error at the `.name`. (4) `.Name` (field or variant) resolves the name exactly as declared, case included (`.Custom`, never `.custom`); no output converts case — a name another system needs is an explicit string (281). | 376 | 14s8-b · (stages) | **Every function has a stage — `Build`, `Run` or `Any` — computed from the resources it uses and propagated through its calls; `use` takes its hook's stage.** (1) **Resources.** Build-only: `decl.*`, `@typeInfo`, `@TypeInfo.all`, a `comptime x: @Expr<T>`'s `.value` / `.fail`, a template body's `q.parts()` / `q.source()`. Run-only: a host function with an effect (io, `fs`, clock, random, network, `process`, `env`), an `await` on a host `@Task`, a module's mutable state (a `keyed: true` `var`). Neither: arithmetic, strings, records, arrays, `case`, loops, std's pure primitives — stage `Any`. (2) **Propagation.** `Any + Any = Any`, `Any + Build = Build`, `Any + Run = Run`; `Build + Run` is an error at the second resource, naming the first (`build-and-run`). A `comptime { … }` block inside a `Run` function is resolved at build into a constant: `val nomes = comptime campos(Post);` in a function that reads the clock is legal. (3) **`use` is a means, not a stage:** `use h(…)` has `h`'s stage — `provide` and `context` are `Any` (a scope push and a lookup, 388), a style's activation `Any`, `request()` / `response()` / `cookieValue(…)` / an atom `Run`, a hook of the program what its body gives. (4) **Host functions are `Run` by default** (67); std declares `Any` on its pure primitives (`string`, `math`, 263) — a library may declare its own; the spelling is the implementing front's. (5) **Uses.** What is computed at build is 380's. A body that runs at build (a decorator's, a template's, a `comptime` block) calls `Build` and `Any` functions — a helper of the program included —, never a `Run` one (`run-in-build`, at the call). `async` (375) exists only inside `Run`. (2) **`modules/std-wasm/`**: std's host functions implemented in Zig, compiled to wasm, served to the comptime runtime and transpiled into the user's `--target wasm` output (440, 442). (3) **`modules/beam-to-wasm/`**: a standalone utility with no role in botopink for now (438, 452). (4) **Resolution at build:** 452 (1) — a `std:` cell, else an `@External.Wasm` `fn:` cell, else refused at the call. (5) **One answer:** a comptime result is std-wasm's answer, whatever the build's target (`string:uppercase` of non-ASCII text, float formatting: one text for the four targets). (6) **First:** front 144's B-00 group opens before any other step of any front; front 14's and 18's open steps move into 144; the wat runtime's limits (`limit 1`…`4`, the core-module subset, `i64` overflow) are the only runtime's limits (depth of `==`: 471). A `std:` naming no std-wasm export, or one whose parameter and result types differ from the declaration's, is a located error at the annotation. (c) (JSPI / stack switching) is not taken: 392 rules out JSPI and the `browser` host has neither. | 509 | B-58 | **One code cache, the CLI's, for every target; what a test exercises never comes from it.** The maintainer, 2026-10-12. (1) **Only dependencies are cached.** The package under test — a cell's own package, the member whose tests run, the project being built or run — is always analysed, generated, compiled and run; its dependencies (std, a `path` or `git` dependency, a library) may come from the cache. (2) **Level A, first:** every target's toolchain step after the codegen, for a dependency's modules, under the key of its input bytes and that tool's version — erlang's `.erl` → `.beam` (the options, the OTP release, the erts / `compiler` / `stdlib` versions; a source with `-include` or `parse_transform` is never looked up), wasm's module assembly and its runtime's precompile, and any later step a target adds; one mechanism for every target; it replaces `cache/erlcheck` and `cache/beam`; `build`, `run`, `test` and `cell check` read it. **Level B, later** and only after the invariant "a package's analysis and generated code depend on its dependency closure alone, never on its consumer" is proved by a test: a dependency's checked interface and generated code per target under its closure key. (3) **The closure key** (a hash): the package's files by content, its dependencies' closure keys (Merkle), the target, and the compiler's sources by backend partition (249's `backend-partition.txt`), never the binary's hash alone. (4) **Where:** 225's cache root — the workspace root, else the project's own. Every `conformance/modules/` holds a workspace manifest whose members are its cells (`"workspaces": ["*"]`), so a library's cells share one cache of their dependencies in `conformance/modules/.botopinkbuild/cache/`. An entry is stored by its hash, never by a package or module name; a program never runs from the cache — an entry is materialised (hard link, else copy) into the project's own output directory. (5) `--cold` on `build`, `run`, `test` and `cell check` reads nothing and writes what it ran; a gate's `--cold` hands it on; CI and a landing run cold. Only a success is stored, nothing when a key cannot be computed with certainty; concurrent writers stage and rename, no lock. (3) `needs` is no configuration: no annotation of the program turns a feature on or off (67); today's fixed `-S http` comes from `needs: [.Http]` on the HTTP bindings. (4) STD-001 refuses at the call, never at the import (503's direction): `std/failure` is importable on every target, a call reaching a function with no wasm form refused, located. A binding that forgets its `needs` fails visibly on the host; a wasmtime cell calling `attempt` keeps it in the gate. Amends 491. | `1-botopink-lang/144` | wasm_attempt_by_exception_handling |
| 519 | B-12-b | **`@embed(comptime path) -> Asset` replaces `@embedFile` / `@embedBytes`; a `Build` builtin, its file embedded in the artifact.** The maintainer, 2026-10-14. `@embedFile` / `@embedBytes` are replaced by the builtin `@embed(comptime path: string) -> Asset`, `Asset` a compiler type like `Bytes`, no import (amends 342, 346's `@embedBytes`). A builtin, not a std function: a std function reading the file system is `Run` (376 (4)), and the reader, the per-target embedding and B-60's key are the compiler's work. `@embed` is a `Build` builtin (376); a call whose arguments are all known at build is computed where it is written, no `comptime` needed (extends 380 (1) from `Any` calls to `Build` calls; a `comptime` written stays legal, 380 (4)); an argument not known at build is an error at the argument. The path resolves against the `botopink.json` of the package that writes it, a path passed on through a `comptime` parameter in its caller's package (342's rules otherwise kept: no absolute path, no `..`, a missing file a located error). The read is a binding served by a reader handed to the compiler: the CLI and the LSP read the disk, `compiler-web` refuses with a located error. The `Asset` (`path`, `bytes`, `size`, `hash`) is a build constant and the artifact carries its copy embedded on every target — an Erlang binary, a JS `Uint8Array`, a wasm data segment; the program never reads the file at run time. `text()` on bytes that are not UTF-8 is a located build error. Every file read joins B-60's closure key, with a case in `test-analysis-equivalence`. | `1-botopink-lang/144` | embed_returns_asset |

Still refused: `Bytes` widening to `unknown` (so `@print(b)`), a `Bytes` template hole, `is` and a type pattern on `Bytes` (on Erlang a `Bytes` and a `string` are the same binary at run time), ordering (`<`, `>`; `==` stays), `+` between `Bytes` and `string` (346: no implicit conversion). Printing is written: `b.length()`, `b.toUtf8()`. A printed form chosen by the static type (`<<104, 105>>` on every target, no widening to `unknown`) is a later decision that only accepts what is refused today. | `1-botopink-lang/144` | bytes_build_constant_only |
| 521 | B-78-a | **commonJS answers std-wasm's text wherever it differs from the JS engine's: an `f64` as text, `trim`, case mapping.** The maintainer, 2026-10-14. (c), for now, widened to every task method: amends 432's methods — `map` and `then` are withdrawn from `@Task<T>`, and `mapError` and `catch` from `@Task<@Result<T, E>>`, so no nested-form declaration is needed and `builtins.d.bp` declares none; a failing task is read only by `try await … catch` or `case` on `await`'s `@Result`, one way to handle a failure; composing without awaiting is an `async { … }` block (375), itself a `@Task` whose value is its tail (blk-a) (`val safe = async { try await load() catch 0 };` is a `@Task<i32>`). A task's value is read by `await`, and every composition — map, bind, recover, map the error — is an `async` block (`async { (try await t catch 0) * 2 }`). The reversed cell is named `task_failure_rejects`. A task method may return by a later decision, a recovering one under a name that is not a keyword. | `1-botopink-lang/144` B-06 · 432 · 375 | task_methods_withdrawn_async_block |
| 524 | B-28-a | **`botopink format` is applied, never checked: the commit hook formats the commit's `.bp` files; the seven rewrites are the canonical form; C-13 becomes formatting.** The maintainer, 2026-10-14. (a), with the formatter as an applier, never a check: the seven rewrites are the canonical form. `botopink format` never fails a gate — the `format-check` stage leaves botopink-lang's gate (`scripts/format-check.sh`) and any library gate or CI that holds one (amends 66). It is applied, never checked: each repository's commit hook runs `botopink format` over the commit's `.bp` files and commits the result — it formats the version entering the commit and never pulls in unstaged changes (the mechanism the implementing step's) —, and the LSP formats on save; no front ends with a reformat step. The three libraries out of the canonical form today (emilia, jhonstart, onze) get one reformat-only commit each, now, with the library's cells and tests green, at a moment when its front holds no open branch. A language rule stays an error of the parser or the checker (`@block`, a `{` without `->` used as a function, a reserved word as a name); the formatter keeps layout and synonyms alone. C-13 becomes formatting: a `;` after the closing brace of a block-shaped statement is accepted by the parser and removed by the formatter — the `blockStatementSemicolon` refusal is withdrawn. | `1-botopink-lang/144` B-28 · `2-libraries/154` · `2-libraries/149` · `2-libraries/162` · 66 | format_applied_canonical_rewrites |
| 525 | blk-a | **A `{` without `->` is a block, never a closure; `@block` is removed; a block used as a value answers its last expression written without `;` (409).** The maintainer, 2026-10-14. In every position, a `{` followed by parameters and `->` (or `->` alone) is a lambda, and a `{` without `->` is a block — a scope, never a closure; a trailing lambda follows the same rule, so one with no parameter writes `f(x) { -> … }`. `@block` is removed (a located refusal whose fix writes `{ … }`; `builtins.d.bp` loses it; the `block_*` cells become its refusal cell and blocks' cells). A block used as a value answers its last expression written without `;` (409), as a function and a lambda do — a bare `{ … }`, a `case` arm `p { … }`, `comptime { … }` (its `break v` removed) and `async { … }` alike. A `return` in a block leaves the enclosing function (a block is a scope); a `return` in `async { … }` leaves the `async` block, a task of its own. A `var` of the enclosing function reassigned in a block is a plain reassignment on every target. A block handed where a function is expected is refused at the block with the hint "expected a function, got a block's value — missing `->`?"; a block whose value is `void` handed where `unknown` is expected is refused. `botopink format` migrates: a trailing lambda without `->` gains it, the last `;` of a `case` arm used as a value and `comptime { break v; }` become a tail. | `1-botopink-lang/144` | brace_without_arrow_is_block |

Steps: a 144 step (the field, a cell with two same-named members of two types), then 150 s30 in rakun. | `1-botopink-lang/144` · `2-libraries/150` | member_decl_names_owner |
| 527 | B-07-b | **`Failure`'s kinds exact on every target: wasm never imitates a host raise; an unmarked raise is `crash`; a failing `assert` and an integer division by zero are `panic`; a host raise's message is `~tp` without the stack.** The maintainer, 2026-10-14. (0) wasm never imitates a host raise: under 518 a JavaScript host (node, browser) raising into wasm through exception handling is a real `host` and answers `host`; on wasmtime, where no host raises, the cell's host case narrows its targets — a private `fn:` body that panics to stand for a host raise is removed. (1) `host` exact as 492 says: every call of a binding without `result: true` is wrapped so its raise is marked `host` on erlang and beam as on commonJS, and an unmarked raise answers `crash`; the per-call cost is measured, and returns to the maintainer if high (492). (2) A failing `assert` is a `panic` whose message names the asserted expression and its location (`assertion failed: x > 0 at src/main.bp:12:5`), the same on every target. (3) An integer division by zero on erlang and beam raises a marked `panic`, as commonJS does — never a bare `badarith` read as `host`. (4) A host raise's `Failure.message` on erlang and beam is the reason printed by `~tp` without the stack — 489's `HostError` rule, one text for both. (5) `std` knows no library: jhonstart's navigation signals stop being raises and become data its renderer reads (504), front 149's work; until then its cell records the gap. | `1-botopink-lang/144` B-07 · `2-libraries/149` · 489 · 492 | failure_kinds_exact_every_target |
| 528 | B-29-a · (process) | **A push to `feat` runs in a CI concurrency group of its own commit, so every commit runs to the end; pull requests keep `cancel-in-progress`.** The maintainer, 2026-10-11. **(a).** A push to `feat` runs in a concurrency group of its own commit — `group: test-${{ github.ref }}-${{ github.event_name == 'push' && github.sha || 'pr' }}`, `cancel-in-progress` kept for pull requests — so every commit on `feat` runs to the end and has its own verdict, which 474 and 477 read. | 529 | props-g | **A component's props are plain parameters or one record parameter `..props: R`; `..` is the one spelling of the rest; a `.bpp` unfolds to `(..props: Props)`.** The maintainer, 2026-10-14. (1) **Plain parameters:** a component (any function) may take its props as ordinary parameters — `pub fn Card(title: string, featured: bool = false) -> View` —, and a tag passes each attribute as the labelled argument of that name (`<Card title="Olá" featured />` is `Card(title: "Olá", featured: true)`); an attribute naming no parameter, or of the wrong type, is a build error at the attribute. (2) **A record parameter:** `..props: R`, `R` a record type (or one derived by 307), makes `R`'s fields the function's parameters from outside — a tag and a call pass them by label — and the body reads them as the record `props`; at most one per function, beside other parameters (`fn Card(..base: CardProps, onClick: fn() -> void)`), a name present in both refused at the declaration. A spread (`Card(..p, featured: true)`, `<Card {..p} featured />`) is legal only into a record parameter, `p` of its type or one derived from it (359's rule); a function of plain parameters takes no spread. (3) **`..` is the one spelling of "the rest"** (amends 359): a record's construction spreads with `..` (`Pessoa(..old, nome: "Ana")`), a tag with `{..p}`, a pattern's rest stays `..`, a variadic parameter stays `..values: T[]`; `...` is only the inclusive range `a...b` (a spread has no left operand). `botopink format` migrates `...` spreads to `..`. (4) The inline `type(…)` parameter (207) stays. (5) A `.bpp` file with `type Props(…)` unfolds to `pub default fn (..props: Props) -> View` (amends 199, 213): its caller passes the fields by label, the body still reads `props.title`. | `1-botopink-lang/144` · `2-libraries/149` · 192 · 199 · 207 · 213 · 359 | component_props_plain_or_rest |

| 531 | B-42-a | **A dependency function wasm cannot lower, which the program never calls: does it stop the build?** The maintainer, 2026-10-14. A call inside a template expansion takes the frame of the template's use, the one line it has — no call of an expansion is left unlocated. | `1-botopink-lang/144` | build_raise_located_with_frames |
| 533 | B-32-a | **`comptime-badarith` names its cause only for a whole-expression `/`** The maintainer, 2026-10-14. | 534 | B-26-a | **`Dict.bump` on a plain `Dict` of non-integer values** The maintainer, 2026-10-14. | 535 | B-41-a | **The text of a run-time abort that is not a panic on erlang and beam** The maintainer, 2026-10-14. An uncaught run-time abort writes to stderr the `Failure` an `attempt` would have caught (527), `<kind>: <message>` — `panic: division by zero` (a run-time division by zero on erlang and beam is a marked `panic`, 527 (3)), `crash: <reason>` for a failing match or any unmarked raise, `host: <message>` for a host raise —, never the raw `Class:Reason` term, as 472 never prints the raw term at build. | 536 | B-64-a | **How a single-file `test/` suite narrows its targets (decision 475 (1); 144 B-64)** The maintainer, 2026-10-14. (a): a `test/` suite that narrows its targets is a project suite — its own directory with a `botopink.json` naming `"targets"`, as a cell does (475 (1)); a single file declares no narrowing, and the `// targets:` line is removed (one place for a restriction, the manifest; the compiler reads no configuration from a comment). The seven suites become seven directories. | `1-botopink-lang/144` | test_suite_narrows_by_manifest |
| 537 | 523-a | **Decision 523's example `async { (try await t catch 0) * 2 }` is refused by `try-await-operand`** The maintainer, 2026-10-14. (c), neither option as written: `try-await-operand` is removed and the precedence is fixed. (1) `await` and `try` are prefix operators, binding as the unary `-` does: `await t * 2` is `(await t) * 2`, `await x + await y` is `(await x) + (await y)`, `try load() + 1` is `(try load()) + 1`. (2) `catch` is an infix operator with `??`'s precedence, below every arithmetic, comparison and logical operator: its right side is evaluated first and is the fallback, its left side is the whole expression before it — `try await t catch 0 * 2` is `(try await t) catch (0 * 2)`, `try await t + 1 catch 0` is `((try await t) + 1) catch 0`. (3) Parentheses group as anywhere: `(try await t catch 0) * 2` doubles the result, so 523's example `async { (try await t catch 0) * 2 }` compiles as written. Every such line has one reading; no refusal remains. Amended (the maintainer, 2026-10-14): postfix (`.`, `()`, `[]`) binds tighter than the prefixes `await` and `try`, as `-t.x` is `-(t.x)` and as JS: `await t.x` is `await (t.x)`, `await f()` is `await (f())`. | `1-botopink-lang/144` | await_try_prefix_catch_infix |
| 538 | B-12-c | **`Asset`'s details 519 leaves open** The maintainer, 2026-10-14. (a), `size` an `i64`, and the `Asset` opens as a `File`. As built: `hash` the SHA-256 of the bytes in lowercase hex; `text()` of an `Asset` not known at build refused (`embed-text-not-at-build`), its message naming `asset.open().text()`; a path through a `comptime` parameter reaches the function as a hidden last parameter `__embed_<p>: Asset`, one body per function. Added (amends 519): `pub fn open(self: Asset) -> File` answers fs-a's `File` over the embedded copy — no `@Result` (the build proved the file exists), every read of `File`'s API (`read`, `bytes`, `text`, `chunks`, `lines`, `hash`), a fallible one answering `@Result<…, FsError>` as on disk, `close` a no-op; code taking a `File` reads a file on disk (`fs.open`) and an embedded one alike (Go's `embed.FS` model). An `Asset` is a module-level constant. | `1-botopink-lang/144` | asset_size_hash_open |
| 539 | defer-a | **`defer`, no resource check** The maintainer, 2026-10-14. (1) `defer <expr>;` runs `<expr>` when the block it is written in ends — block scope (525), as Zig and Swift. (2) Several run in reverse order. (3) It runs on every exit of the block: the end, `return`, `break`, `continue`, a propagating `try`, a `throw`, a panic `attempt` catches. (4) In an `async` body it runs when the task's body ends, after its awaits; in a `@Stream` when the stream ends or its consumer stops (a `break` in `for await`). (5) Its body cannot leave the path: `return`, `break` / `continue` out of it, `yield` and `await` in it are refused, located. Lowering: commonJS `try { … } finally { … }` (and the iterator's `return()`), erlang / beam `try … after … end`, wasm the code at each exit and 518's exception handling. (6) No check (as Go, Zig, Java, Node, Erlang): a `Closeable` value not closed compiles, and so does one held by a module-level `val`; closing is the writer's, with `defer`. std declares `pub behavior Closeable { fn close(self: Self); }`, a plain behavior for generic code, known to no compiler rule — the compiler names no std type and holds no error tied to one. Amended (the maintainer, 2026-10-14): the deferred expression is evaluated when it runs, not when `defer` is written (Zig's rule: `var f = open("a"); defer f.close(); f = open("b");` closes `b`), and a `return <expr>`'s value is computed before the block's defers run. | `1-botopink-lang/144` | defer_runs_at_block_end |
| 540 | fs-a | **`io.fs` redesigned on Rust's, Java's, Erlang's and Node's common shape** The maintainer, 2026-10-14. (1) **Typed error:** `pub type FsError { NotFound(path), PermissionDenied(path), IsDirectory(path), NotDirectory(path), AlreadyExists(path), InvalidUtf8(path, offset: i64), Other(path, message) }` — the same OS error the same variant on every target (`ENOENT`, `enoent`, WASI's code → `NotFound`); `Other.message` is 489's text. Every `io.fs` function answers `@Result<…, FsError>`, never a `string` error. (2) **Whole-file shortcuts:** `readBytes`, `readText` (UTF-8 checked: `InvalidUtf8` on every target — never node's silent U+FFFD nor erlang's unchecked binary), `writeBytes`, `writeText`, `appendText`, `stat` (`size: i64`, `mtime: i64`, `isDir: bool`), and the directory functions (`exists`, `list`, `mkdir`, `mkdirRecursive`, `rm`, `removeTree`, `copy`, `walk`, `glob`). (3) **The handle:** `open(path) -> @Result<File, FsError>` (read), `create(path)` (write, creating or truncating); `File implement Closeable` with `path()`, `size() -> @Result<i64, FsError>`, `read(n: i32)` (up to `n` bytes, empty at the end), `bytes()`, `text()`, `chunks(size: i32 = 65536) -> @Stream<@Result<Bytes, FsError>>`, `lines() -> @Stream<@Result<string, FsError>>`, `hash()` (SHA-256, lowercase hex), `write(b: Bytes)`, `close()`; closed by the writer with defer-a's `defer` (`val f = try fs.open(p); defer f.close();`), unchecked. (4) **Every target:** commonJS `fs.*Sync`, erlang / beam `file:*` (`[read, binary]`, `read` / `read_line` in a loop for the streams), wasm `wasi:filesystem` with `read-via-stream` + `wasi:io/streams` driven by 392's scheduler — wasm gains bindings it has none of today. Amended (the maintainer, 2026-10-14): the wasm bindings declare `needs: [.Filesystem]` (518); on the browser host, which has no filesystem, a call reaching `io.fs` is refused at the call, located, as 518 refuses `std/failure`'s wasm-less functions (503's direction) — the import stays legal. | `1-botopink-lang/144` | fs_typed_error_shape |
| 541 | 525-a | **What decision 525 leaves open about blocks** The maintainer, 2026-10-14. (a) for each, with a migration first. (1) A lambda answers its last expression written without `;` (409, 525): `{ x -> x + 1; }` answers nothing; before the rule takes effect `botopink format` removes, once, the `;` of every existing lambda's last expression statement, so no program changes meaning (`xs.map({ x -> x + 1; })` would otherwise become an array of `void` with no error). (2) `await`, `yield`, and `break` / `continue` to an outer loop inside a block reach the enclosing function or loop — a block is a scope, never a closure. (3) A `case` arm's `break <value>` (decision 2) is removed, as `comptime`'s was: the arm's value is its tail. (4) A block as the last statement of a function body is its tail. (5) The formatter rewrites a trailing lambda with no parameter as `{ -> … }`, as built. Amended (the maintainer, 2026-10-14): 524's one-off reformat of emilia, jhonstart and onze runs after the formatter carries these rewrites and 529's `...` → `..`, so each library is reformatted once. | `1-botopink-lang/144` | lambda_answers_last_expression |
| 542 | B-30-b | **Wiring the wasm heap into the codegen: generic slots, task records, collection points** The maintainer, 2026-10-14. Q1 (a), Q2 (b) widened to every target, Q3 (a′), Q4 the same refusal everywhere. Q1: every body whose layout depends on a type parameter is specialized by whether that parameter is a pointer or not — at most two versions per type parameter, never one per type —, so every block header names its pointer words exactly. Q2: a `@Task` record lives in its creator's heap; a task handle never crosses heaps — a message holding a `@Task` is refused at the send, located, on every target (an erlang or commonJS program that sends a task is refused as the wasm one is, never a wasm-only departure); a settled value reaches the awaiter copied into its heap at the `await` (erlang's model: only the owner awaits). Q3 (a′): a collection runs at a task's suspension and at the scheduler's turns, where the frame is saved, and also at the end of each iteration of a loop that allocates, where the compiler saves the live pointer locals at that point only — a function with no loop, and a loop that does not allocate, pay nothing; a long synchronous loop never exhausts memory on wasm where erlang and commonJS finish (434 (5)). Q4: a value holding a cycle sent to a task is refused with `message contains a cycle` on every target, the same text. Amended (the maintainer, 2026-10-14): Q3 also collects at a call to a recursive function (direct, or in a call cycle), saving the live pointer locals at those call sites only — Erlang-style recursion allocating without a loop or an `await` never exhausts memory; only recursive calls pay. | 547 | 17-c | **What else names a `keyed: true` var** The maintainer, 2026-10-14. None of the three: the default alone, for now. A module-level `var` stays, its data immutable (`counts = counts.insert(k, v)` is Erlang's `Counts2 = maps:put(…)`), and without any annotation it is **one value for the whole program on every target** (amends 430's per-process default on the BEAM): BEAM a one-row ETS table, commonJS a variable, wasm a permanent root outside every heap (522 (6)). `@BeamMemory` keeps two members only, a closed enum as 518's `WasmNeed`, in 302's shape: `#[@BeamMemory(.Ets)]` — the default, a one-row ETS table, the holding with no pathological case — and `#[@BeamMemory(.PersistentTerm)]` for a `var` written rarely and read much (a write scans every process; the cost is the reference's to document and the writer's to weigh, no check); any other value is refused at the annotation. Amended (the maintainer, 2026-10-14): (i) on the BEAM the program's runtime, at boot (`botopink run`'s `main`, the application's start), creates every module `var`'s ETS table in a process that lives as long as the program and runs the initializers once — a table never belongs to the first process that touches the `var`, so it never dies with it; on commonJS and wasm the initializer runs at module load. (ii) A reassignment that reads the `var` it writes (`x = f(x)`) is atomic on the BEAM — read, compute, `ets:select_replace` against the value read, retried when another process wrote between —, so no write is lost and the answer is commonJS's and wasm's; a plain `x = v` is one insert. (iii) A module `var` whose type holds a `@Task` is refused, located, on every target (a task never crosses heaps, B-30-b). | 553 | mark-a, 541-c | **A body that compiles specially carries a marker; the return type is written whole, and a failure is always a `Result` in the value's layer** The maintainer, 2026-10-15. (1) The markers, before `fn` and before a lambda's brace: `iter` (`yield`), `async` (`await`), `async iter` (`yield` and `await`), `component` (`use`, `await`) — `pub async fn`, `iter fn() -> Iter<i32> { … }`, `iter { -> yield 1 }`, `async { x -> await f(x) }`; `->` tells a lambda from the block forms `async { … }` (523) and `iter loop { … }` (125); no other combination; a function named `iter`, `async` or `component` called with a trailing lambda writes its parentheses (525), each word a name elsewhere. (2) The return type is written whole, named after its marker: `Iter<T, R = void>` and `AsyncIter<T, R = void>` (`T` each `yield`'s value, `R` the value the iteration ends with — the body's `return v` — as JS's `Generator<T, TReturn>`; `next()` answers `Step.Item(T)` or `Step.Done(R)`), `Async<T>`, `Component<R>`; `@Iterator`, `@Task`, `@Stream` and `@Component` are renamed; a marker whose return type is not its type is refused (`marker-type-mismatch`). (3) A failure is a `Result` in the value's layer on every type: `Result<T, E>`; `Async<Result<T, E>>` (432); `Iter<T, Result<V, E>>` / `AsyncIter<T, Result<V, E>>` — a `throw` or a propagating `try` ends the iteration with `Error(e)`, the items bare `T`, consumed by `for x in try xs` / `for await x in try xs`; `Component<Result<R, E>>` — a component throws only under it, the `Error` reaching the nearest `ErrorBoundary` (414, 504). A propagating `try` and a `throw` are written only where that layer is a `Result`, and there `return v` is `Ok(v)`; a `try … catch` handled in the body needs none (537); `Iter<Result<T, E>>` stays an iterator of results (`yield Error(e)` goes on). `Result` needs no marker: a failure is an early return and changes nothing of how a body runs. (4) Refused, located: the keyword without its marker (the hint naming it); a `throw` or a propagating `try` where the layer is not a `Result`; a marked lambda whose expected type differs, or with none and no annotation (147). (5) A function that hands a value on writes the same signature with no marker; a component's asynchrony stays inferred (375). (6) Three pieces, each one landing per repository: the markers (the `@` types kept meanwhile, every cell the same); the types (the `@` removed, the names changed, declared in std's `.bp`, a codemod, every cell the same); what is new (an iterator's `R` and `Step`, a failure ending an iteration, `Component<Result<R, E>>`, on the four backends, new cells). Answers 541-c: a lambda or `fn` expression is a generator when it carries `iter`, its type agreeing with its expected type (147). A declaration writes its annotations first, then `pub`, then `default`, then its marker (`iter`, `async`, `async iter`, `component`), then `fn`: `#[serverAction] pub async fn count(…)`, `pub default async fn load()`, `pub async iter fn lines(…)` — as Rust's `pub async fn` and JavaScript's `export async function`. One order only: any other (`async pub fn`) is a parse error at the marker; on a lambda the marker sits right before the brace (`async { x -> … }`). The formatter and the codemod write this order | `1-botopink-lang/144` B-116 | marker_order_before_fn |

| 575 | (the maintainer, 2026-10-16–17, case by case) | **No declaration files; a builtin is a std declaration marked `#[builtin]`; a `declare fn` names its host sources with `#[compiler.external(…)]`; a build-time body talks to the compiler through the keyword `compiler`; `comptime` and `syntax` on a parameter say how an argument arrives.** (1) **No `.d.bp`.** The declaration-file kind goes — `DECLARATION_EXT`, `Module.declaration`, every reader —; every `.d.bp` is renamed `.bp` or dissolved: `builtins.d.bp` and `builtins_fns.d.bp` into the std modules that own each declaration; rakun's `rakun.d.bp` (declares nothing) deleted with its `files` entry; the conformance cells' renamed (`files_one_module_twice` keeps its one-file-listed-twice form). A file still ending in `.d.bp` is a module named `x.d`, nothing special. Replaces 252, 514, 515; amends 66. (2) **`#[builtin]`.** `@name` is a std declaration marked `#[builtin]` — `pub fn builtin(syntax decl: unknown) -> void { if (!compiler.isStd) compiler.fail("…", at: decl); compiler.setBuiltin(true); }` —, each in the std module where it belongs (`Result` in `result.bp`, `embed` and `Asset` in `asset.bp` over `io.fs.File`, `src` and `typeInfo` in `reflection.bp`, `Compiler` in `compiler.bp`, `print` in `io.bp`, `panic` and `todo` in `panic.bp`); inside its own module the name is written without `@`; two std declarations marking one name `builtin-twice`, outside std `setBuiltin-outside-std`. (3) **`compiler`, a keyword.** In a body that runs at build — a decorator, a template, a function called at build, a `comptime` block — `compiler` is the `Compiler` of that point: the annotated declaration in a decorator, the call site elsewhere; a `Build` resource (376 (1)), `compiler-at-run-time` in a run-time-only body; never a parameter (`src()`, `embed(path)`, `typeInfo(t)` take none). `Compiler` is a `behavior` of std whose one implementor is the compiler — no `#[Intrinsic]`, no `Implementation.Compiler`; a unit test holds the compiler's table of its members to `compiler.bp` —: `src`, `module`, `package`, `isStd`; `fail(message, syntax at: ?unknown = null)` (at the argument or the declaration `at` holds, else at `src`; the body goes on); the outputs, a decorator's only (`compiler-output-outside-decorator`): `compile(syntax executor: fn())` — run-time code compiled beside the annotated declaration, run once before `main` in the program's init list: modules in dependency order, declarations in source order, calls in order; any number per declaration —, `setMeta<M>(syntax value: M)` (298, one per `M`), `addMember<F>(name, syntax member: F)` (370 (2)'s typed form, the only one: the string form goes), `addType(name, syntax source: type)`, `implement(source: Implementation)` (one per target, `implementation-twice`; on a declaration with a body `implement-with-body`), `setBuiltin(on)`; the reads: `embed(path) -> Bytes` (519's checks), `catalogue(with, member = null)` (253; its entries declaration handles, read at build, 576 (7)), `record(fields: compiler.Field<unknown>[]) -> type` (307: named by the module-level `val` that binds the answer, `derived-type-outside-val`, `derived-type-cycle`, `record-field-duplicate`; a field that `fields` produced keeps its source's default and markers, 423), `fields<T>(t: type<T>) -> compiler.Field<T>[]`, `optional<T>(f)` / `required<T>(f)` over a field (`?F` / `F`, source kept), `typeInfo<T>(t: type<T>) -> TypeInfo<T>`, `text(syntax e: unknown) -> string`, `name(syntax e: unknown) -> ?string` (`null` for an expression with no name, 643). Replaces 216's four places; amends 298. (4) **`comptime` and `syntax` on a parameter; no `Expr<T>` type.** `comptime x: T`: a value known at build, the body holds a `T`. `syntax x: T`: the expression written, captured as a tree, opaque in the body — handed only to `compiler.*` or into another tree; a decorator's first `syntax` is the annotated declaration, filled by the compiler and checked against the parameter's type (280 (2)). `-> syntax T`: a template's return, the return expression captured and spliced at the call (replaces `@expr`). An `syntax` argument to an `syntax` parameter hands its tree on. `@Expr<T>`, `.value` and `Decl<T>` go (reverts 364 (4); replaces 364 (1), (2) and 370 (1)); `Declared<T>` goes too (576 (7)). **The tree rule:** a tree — an executor, a meta value, a member, a template's return — holds exactly run-time code (names resolved where written, 384), `syntax` (spliced as the expression held) and `comptime` (parameters and the body's `val`s, spliced as literals); a `compiler.*` call inside one is evaluated at build and spliced as its answer (614; its argument a build value, else `compiler-arg-at-run-time`); a `-> syntax T` function is called only by the template syntax, an annotation, or inside a tree (`syntax-call-outside-syntax`). (5) **`type<T>`.** The type of the type `T`, the generic form of `type`: `comptime t: type<T>` takes a type and binds `T` (`@typeInfo(city)` with a value is refused); replaces `type T` at its seven sites; `type(` / `type {` stay the inline type; opaque, read through `compiler.fields` / `compiler.typeInfo`. (6) **A `declare fn` names its sources.** A bodyless `declare fn` — top-level or a type's, static included — is refused at the declaration, `declare-without-implementation`, unless an annotation gave it an implementation; a target without one is refused at the call as today (146; amends 311 (1)). `external` is one std function, `#[builtin] pub fn external(syntax decl: unknown, comptime ..sources: Implementation[]) -> void { for (s in sources) compiler.implement(s); }`, `Implementation` the enum `Erlang(template, inline = false, result = false) | JavaScript(template, result = false) (618, 619) | Wasm(template | op | std | wasi — one —, host, needs, result)`, written `#[@external(.Erlang("…"), .JavaScript("…"), .Wasm(std: "…"))]` (no `@` inside std); the shape errors are the enum constructor's; the compiler reads `Implementation` alone — `external_variants`, `translateExternalCells` and the backends' re-parse of the prelude go, the table checks (`fn:`, `op:`, `std:`, `wasi:`, `result:`, `host:` twice, 516) stay over the value —; a library writes its own source annotation the same way (`#[bif("lists")]` over `compiler.implement`). `#[@Host]`, `declare` inside a `behavior` (`declare-in-behavior`) and the delegate forms (`val x = declare fn`, the unannotated shorthand) go. `print`, `println` and `debug` are bound by their cells on every target (`.Wasm(std: "println")`); no backend intercepts them. Amends 238, 305, 481, 516, 518; 445 keeps `std:` std's. (7) **Bodies in std.** What the compiler lowered by name is written in botopink: `Result`'s five methods (`case` over `Ok` / `Error`; the backends' inline lowering and `comptime/builtins.zig`'s `methods` rows go); `src()` (`return compiler.src`); `embed(comptime path: string) -> Asset` (`compiler.embed`; `Asset(path, size, hash, file: File)`, `text()` = `self.file.text()`, run time on every target; `File.ofBytes` replaces `fs.openAsset`; `Asset.open()`, `embed-text-not-utf8` and `embed-text-not-at-build` go — amends 519, 538); `typeInfo(comptime t: type<T>)` (`compiler.typeInfo`); `TypeInfo.all` goes (576 (7)); `types.bp` whole: `compiler.Field<T>(name, type: type, annotations)` (`type` for `typeName`; a `type` value at run time is its name), `of` / `all` over `compiler.fields(T)` (computed at build, 380), `optional()` / `required()`, `keys` (`return Field<T>`), `partial` / `required` / `merge` / `pick` / `omit` over `compiler.fields` + `compiler.record` (`derived_types.zig` keeps only the record's materialisation and the two `val` rules; amends 307, 308). | 577 | 14s8-a · (the maintainer, 2026-10-17) | **A template is called one way: its name before a template text `` @`…` ``, raw.** (1) **The template text** `` @`…` `` is a token of its own: it may span lines, holds its bytes as written — no escape sequences but `` \` `` for a backtick, no interpolation — and is the only literal a template takes; `${…}` and `{…}` inside it are the sub-language's own syntax, parsed by the template from its raw text (14s8-a (e): a hole's expression is spliced into the code the template answers and typed in the caller's scope; the template never reads its value). (2) A `-> syntax T` function — a template — takes exactly that text, its first `syntax string` parameter (616); as an annotation it takes a second `syntax` parameter, the annotated declaration the compiler fills (576). (3) It is called only as `` name @`…` `` — in run-time code or inside a tree — or as an annotation `` #[name @`…`] ``; `name "…"`, `name """…"""`, `name(…)`, `name<T>(…)`, `#[name(…)]` and the template taken as a value are `syntax-call-outside-syntax` (narrows 575 (4)'s three places). (4) A `-> syntax T` function declared with any other parameters is refused at its declaration (`template-signature`); such a function becomes an ordinary function or takes its input as the text. A template is a function whose first parameter — after an `syntax self: R` receiver, for a template method — is `syntax q: string`, the template text (577); it is called only as `` name @`…` ``, `` recv.name @`…` `` (a template method) or `` #[name @`…`] ``; an annotation's second `syntax` parameter is the annotated declaration (576). (2) **`anyvalue`** is a value whose type is not known until its code is built; **`anytype`** is a type not known until it is built, and is the bare `type` — the type of any type — renamed: `comptime t: anytype`, `compiler.Field<T>(…, type: anytype, …)`, `addType(name, syntax source: anytype)`; `type<T>` stays the type of `T` (575 (5)); `type(` / `type {` stay the inline type. | 593 | 04s12-a · (the maintainer, 2026-10-11) | **(c), and a type may be a component — every form behaves and is called as a `fn` component.** (amended by 737: (2)'s "one record parameter, never a list" and the refusal of a second parameter are gone — a `fn` component takes plain parameters or one record, 529 (1), (2)) (1) A method, a lambda or a `default fn` answering `Component<R>` lowers as a top-level component: a lambda over the `RenderScope` (388) answering its tree and its children's scope, with `use provide` / `use context` / `await` in its body, marked by its own body (s23-j); calling it only makes the lambda and marks no caller. (3) Each has its own hooks node, and a method's or a type's node enters `decl.hooks` (277 amended: a method call followed through its receiver's type), so a decorator sees `Menu`'s hooks as it sees `fn App`'s. **(2) in detail** (the maintainer, 2026-10-12): a type implementing `Component<R>` is sugar for a `fn` component. Its fields are the parameters — defaults, optionals, `children`, generics as in a `fn`, immutable — and its `fn render(self: Self) -> Component<R>` is the body, `self` reading the fields and the type's helper methods. `X(fields…)` and `<X …/>` answer the component (the lambda `(props) => (scope) => …`), never a record, so a caller cannot tell the two forms apart. The same lowering on commonJS, erlang, beam and wasm, the same `.d.ts` (`export declare function X(props: {…}): Component<R>`), the same marks (s23-j: a `render` with `await` is asynchronous), the same `decl.hooks` for decorators (`#[page] type` and `#[page] fn` interchangeable), the same comptime (434-b). Hooks only in `render`; a helper method not answering `@Component` is an ordinary function. Refused, located: `implement Component` with no `render`; a `render` of another signature; a `RenderScope` field (388 (5)); a `use` outside `render`; `X.render()` called directly (`call the component, not its body: <X …/> or X(…)`). **(2) amended — a component's props are one record** (the maintainer, 2026-10-12): a component, `fn` or type, takes its props as one record parameter, never a list of parameters. `type MenuProps(items: string[], title: string = "Menu");` — then `fn Menu(props: MenuProps) -> Component<Element> { val t = use context(Theme); return <nav class={t.cls}><h2>{title}</h2><ul>{items.map({ i -> <li>{i}</li> })}</ul></nav>; }`, or the same component as a type: `type Menu(props: MenuProps) implement Component<Element> { fn render(self: Self) -> Component<Element> { …<h2>{self.title}</h2><ul>{self.items.map({ i -> self.item(i) })}</ul>… } fn item(self: Self, i: string) -> Element { return <li>{i}</li>; } }` (`item` does not answer `@Component`: an ordinary helper). The record's fields are in scope: bare in the `fn` body (`title`, `items`), on `self` in `render` and the helpers (`self.title`, `self.items`); `props` names the whole record. Defaults, optionals, `children` and generics are the record's, written once on `MenuProps`. A caller never builds the record: the attributes are its fields — `<Menu items={["a", "b"]}/>` (`title` taking its default), `<Menu items={["a", "b"]} title="Principal"/>`, `<Layout view={ { -> <Menu items={xs}/> } }/>` (inside a lambda prop alike) — and the compiler builds the `MenuProps` from them, checked field by field as a record construction is (a missing field, an unknown attribute, a wrong type: located on the attribute). A component with no props takes no parameter (`fn App() -> Component<Element>`). The lowering is unchanged — `(props) => (scope) => …`, `props` the record — and the `.d.ts` names it: `export declare function Menu(props: MenuProps): Component<Element>`, `MenuProps` exported beside it. Refused, located: a component with more than one parameter, or whose one parameter is not a record type (`a component takes its props as one record: type MenuProps(…); fn Menu(props: MenuProps)`). | `04-js` step 12 · `1-botopink-lang/144` B-17, B-116 · `2-libraries/150` | every_form_component |

[];`, `val pixel = comptime compiler.embed("assets/pixel.bin");`. A node is asynchronous when its body writes `await` or `async { … }`; when it `use`s an asynchronous hook or calls an asynchronous component, whether or not it writes `await`; when it calls a host function answering `@Task`; or when it calls what the checker cannot follow (a function value, a method, a `use` with `hook: null`) — the conservative answer. A cycle is asynchronous when any node in it is. | 332 | jsi64-a | **Big numbers, and `Json` read like Jackson's strict configuration.** (1) `bigint` is a primitive integer of any size with every integer operator and the literal suffix `n` (`123…890n`); erlang/beam use the VM's integer, commonJS `BigInt`, wasm std-wasm's `bigint` (442) — one value on the four targets (front `01-compiler/139`). (2) std's `Decimal` is the exact decimal (Java's `BigDecimal`: an unscaled `bigint` and a scale): `add`, `sub`, `mul` exact; `div` takes a scale and a rounding mode (`a.div(b, scale: 2, rounding: .HalfEven)`) and has no form without them. (3) `==` on `Decimal` compares the numeric value (`1.0 == 1.00`, as `compareTo`); the scale is kept and `toString()` writes it (`"1.00"`). (4) `Json` (the `json` library, 396) chooses numbers by the numeral, as Jackson's nodes with `USE_BIG_DECIMAL_FOR_FLOATS` on: an integer within `i64` is `Int(value: i64)`, a larger one `BigInt(value: bigint)`, one with a fraction or an exponent `Dec(value: Decimal)`, exact; `Num(value: f64)` goes; readers: `num() -> ?f64` (any number, approximate), `i64() -> ?i64`, `bigint() -> ?bigint`, `decimal() -> ?Decimal` (exact, `null` when the value does not fit — never a coercion), `isNumber()`, `isIntegral()`. Writing keeps the value exact: `Int` and `BigInt` as their digits, `Dec` as its plain text with its scale. (5) `#[validated]`'s bind reads a field by its own type, as Jackson's typed binding with `ACCEPT_FLOAT_AS_INT` and `ALLOW_COERCION_OF_SCALARS` off: `i64` exact (a fraction or an out-of-range integer is a violation at the field, never truncated), `bigint` any integer, `Decimal` any number exactly, `f64` any number rounded; a string becomes a number only under `#[coerce]` (325 step 6) | `01-compiler/139` · `02/97` step 15 · `03-bundled-libs/125` · `04-js` step 9 · the 13 files with a `case` over `Json` | bigint_decimal_strict_json |

| 337 | 138-a | **The shorthand import goes; `mod m;` binds the namespace `m` in the module that declares it.** (1) An import names where its names come from: a module of the package by its path (`import {config.splitPath};`, `import {config};` for the namespace — 206, 309) or a package with `from` (206). (2) `mod config;` and `pub mod config;` bind `config` as a namespace in the module that declares them — `config.splitPath(x)` with no import. The namespace walks a folder module's tree as 110's does: with `src/mod1/mod.bp` declaring `pub mod mod2;`, `mod mod1;` alone allows `mod1.mod2.splitPath(x)` — each step a `pub mod` and the leaf `pub` (a plain `mod` stays private to its declaring module's subtree: `mod1.mod2` from outside `mod1` is refused at `mod2`, `private-module`). A sibling or nested module reaches `config` only by import. In the declaring module `import {config};` is refused (`redundant-module-import`, fix: delete the item); a top-level declaration named like a declared module is `import-name-collision`. (3) No combined form (`pub mod config {splitPath};` is not syntax) and no re-export syntax (no `pub use`, no `export … from`): a folder's `mod.bp` exposes a submodule's function with a value, `mod mod2; pub val splitPath = mod2.splitPath;`, the submodule kept private so the name has one public path. (3) **Crashes:** `@panic` and an unannounced host raise are defects, never in a signature; `import {failure.attempt, failure.Failure} from "std"; val r = attempt({ -> plugin(x) });` answers `@Result<T, Failure>` (`Failure(message, kind)`, kind `panic` / `host` / `crash`), the one way to contain one — replacing jhonstart's `__jhTryValue` / `__jhTryTask` (414's renderer) and `testing.asserts`' `tryCatch` Amended by 489 (`HostError`'s fields) and 490 (`throws: true` is `result: true`, the only conversion; a host returning `{error, R}` included) | `01-checker` step 42 · `02/97` step 18 · backends 02–05 · `05-jhonstart/26` step 14 · `01-compiler/24` step 2 | result_is_checked_error |

A crash in a `@Task<T>` is a defect, contained by `attempt` (431). **Methods**, one spelling each (67): `map` and `then` (the bind) on every `@Task<T>`; on `@Task<@Result<T, E>>` also `mapError(f)` and `catch(f)` (recovering into `@Task<T>`); no `flatMap`. (2) **`#[repository]` is only `dbcontext`'s generator on a `behavior`** (313, 398) — the core keeps no `#[repository]` (closes `erk-c`). (3) **One way to provide a bean:** `#[provides]` on a free function reading 299's typed config (`#[provides] fn clock(cfg: AppConfig) -> Clock`); `#[configuration]` and `#[bean]` go (closes `130-c`); qualified beans are 321. (4) **The HTTP client is a generator on a behavior like the repository:** `#[httpClient(EchoConfig)] behavior EchoService { #[get("/users/${id}")] fn user(self: Self, id: string) -> @Result<User, HttpError>; }` — the group a typed config record (299), path holes the method's parameters (311's template form where the spelling needs it); `#[httpExchange]` / `#[getExchange]`… go. (6) **One controller:** `#[controller]`; the return type decides the answer — a `View` is HTML, a record JSON, a `Response` as is; `#[restController]` goes. (7) **Added behaviour is a wrapper (316):** `#[transactional]`, `#[secured(…)]`, `#[retryable(…)]`, `#[useCache]` on a function or method; the hand-written proxies (`<Type>Tx`, `<Type>Sec`) go as each owning front reaches them. ## jhonstart & templates

| # | Id | Rule in force | Binds | Cell |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 186 | 49-f · 03r-ai | The stage a page renders in is a compile-time fact. Stages, in order: **comptime** (the build, where a page is prerendered) → **server** (request time) → **client** (the browser). A hook with no marker may run at any stage; a marker only restricts it, and the compiler detects and validates the `use` of a marked hook. **`#[serverOnly]`** — the server at request time (`searchParams`, cookies, headers, a per-request query): a page that reaches one is rendered per request — the definition of a dynamic page. **`#[clientOnly]`** — the browser only, legal only inside a `#[client]` component; written on a component's tag it renders that instance only on the client (278). Refused at compile time, at the `use`: a `#[serverOnly]` hook inside a `#[client]` component; a `#[clientOnly]` hook outside one; a `#[serverOnly]` hook in a page that declares itself prerendered (`ctr-l`). A page that reaches neither is prerendered. The build writes each route's kind (`routing`'s `k` blob, `pattern\|S\|D`) and rakun only reads it: `markDynamic` in jhonstart's `router.bp`, `rakun_ssr.erl`'s implicit mark and every channel between them go. The checker capability is 277's | 01-checker · 05-jhonstart/26 step 8 · 07-onze/49 step 5 · 04-rakun/22 step 4 | render_stage_compile_time_fact |
| 190 | 08-c | The library decides which types an embedded expression may have: the template function accepts or refuses it with a comptime error located at the expression; the compiler fixes no list. The compiler owes: an embedded expression of any type reaches the template function with its type readable at comptime and its source position, and the function can raise a located comptime error (a `language-gaps.md` row, 01-compiler). The surface is `{expr}`; until the means exist, 118 step 2 reads `{expr}` out of the literal's raw text and re-emits it through `build` (a wrong type is the checker's error at the template call), moving to the typed form without changing the surface | 08-bpp/118 step 2 · 01-compiler | template_decides_embedded_types |
| 200 | 08-a3 | `html` is the `pub default fn` of the core member `jhonstart`, carrying `bpp`'s `#[html]` (`#[bpp.html]`, 361) — `import html, {Element, renderToString} from "jhonstart";` — and the member `jhonstart-html` is deleted (187's criterion); an application's manifest reads `"bpp": "jhonstart"` (361); the examples that depended on `jhonstart-html` import from the core | 05-jhonstart · 08-bpp/116 | html_default_fn_of_jhonstart |
| 202 | 08-g | No page declares or forces its stage: jhonstart's `#[page]` reads through 186's capability whether the page reaches a `#[serverOnly]` hook by `use`; a page that reaches none is prerendered at comptime by that decorator, one that reaches one is rendered per request. No `pub val prerender`, no `output` key in `onze.json`, no way to force a page that reaches no server hook to render per request (290) | 08-bpp/117 step 2 · 124 · 05-jhonstart/26 step 8 | page_stage_from_server_hooks |
