| 351 | props-d | **A native tag's attributes are typed the way React types its intrinsic elements, in botopink's own shapes, and `prelude.bp` is the intrinsic table.** (1) Each native tag is a jhonstart builder whose first parameter is the element's props record — the rule of a component (192), so `html` lowers a native tag as it lowers a component tag: `<a hreff="/x">` is a comptime error at `hreff`, `<input tabIndex="abc">` at the value, content inside a tag whose props declare no `children` (a void element) at the tag. (2) The props are records with defaults layered with `Type.merge` (307), as React's `HTMLAttributes` → `AnchorHTMLAttributes`: `GlobalAttrs` (`id`, `class`, `style`, `title`, `lang`, `dir`, `hidden`, `tabIndex`, …), `AriaAttrs`, and each element's own — `pub val AnchorProps = Type.merge(Type.merge(GlobalAttrs, AriaAttrs), AnchorAttrs);`. A field is `?T = null` (absent when null) or a `bool` (written bare, absent when false); a number is `i32` / `f64`, a closed set an enum (`target={.Blank}`, `type={.Email}`), the rest `string`; a native element's content is its builder's `children` field. (3) Names: a one-word attribute keeps HTML's name; a multi-word one is camelCase as in React (`tabIndex`, `readOnly`, `httpEquiv`, `acceptCharset`), the renderer writing HTML's (`tabindex`, `http-equiv`); `aria-*` are typed fields of `AriaAttrs` (`aria-label` is `ariaLabel`); `data-*` is the one open family, any suffix, a `string` value. (4) **Declared through the prelude** (270, 361): a native tag exists in markup only when the prelude names its builder; a tag it does not name — a custom element (`<my-widget>`), an SVG tag not yet listed — is an error at the tag, written as an app's own component or `el(…)` in a hole; a new tag is its builder, its props and one prelude line. (5) Spread on an element takes a value of that element's props type or of a type derived from it (307), with 359's order and completeness rules (`<a {...linkProps} class="x">`); the `Array<#(string, string)>` pairs leave the builders (`el(…)` keeps them, the escape hatch). An event attribute (`onClick`) is refused at its name until a front declares typed events | 118 steps 1, 4 · `05-jhonstart/26` (`elements.bp`, `prelude.bp`) · 119 (`class` with `#[styled]`) · 120 | native_tag_props_typed |
| 360 | props-e | **Slots work as Astro's, in botopink's annotation form — never props.** (1) **Reading, in the component:** `<Slot />` renders the default slot; `<Slot name="footer">fallback</Slot>` renders the `footer` slot, its content the fallback shown when nothing fills it. `name="footer"` declares the slot — a component's slots are the `<Slot>`s its template writes, no props field, no other declaration. (2) **Filling, at the call:** a direct child annotated `#[slot("footer")]` goes to that slot; several go as a list, in order; `<Fragment #[slot("footer")]>…</Fragment>` fills it with several nodes; an unannotated child goes to the default slot. (3) **Transfer:** `<Slot name="footer" #[slot("footer")] />` inside another component's tag passes this component's `footer` on. (4) **Presence:** `val has = use hasSlot("footer");` → `bool`, a `use` under 357. (5) **`Slot` and `slot` are jhonstart functions with `comptime` parameters**, evaluated when `html` expands, in 302's shape — `pub fn slot(comptime decl: @Decl, comptime name: @Expr<string>)` records the child's target as typed meta (298), `pub fn Slot(comptime decl: @Decl, comptime name: @Expr<string> = "default")` records the slot on the enclosing component's `@Decl` and marks where its content renders; `html` reads both metas by type, the compiler knows no slot (113, 198). Refused at the line of the prelude module: anything but an `import` and its marker `val`; an item of another package (242); an activation (`X*`). An alias is allowed. **The header wins**, by scope order: a name the header declares or imports never reaches the prelude; a tag that then resolves to the header's declaration and is not a builder fails at the tag, naming the header line. | 338 | 08-d | **CSS is built in three layers — `css`, `styled`, emilia — none of which knows `.bpp`; jhonstart integrates them, and a `.bpp` file's style is a section of its own.** (1) **`css`**, the base for building CSS: its own repository, `botopink/css` at `repository/css` (326; std only). A reader of a stylesheet — rules, at-rules, comments, strings, `{ }` nesting — into a typed `Sheet`, its renderer, and `scope(id: string, css: string) -> @Result<string, string>`, which appends `[data-s="<id>"]` to each selector's compounds, leaves `:global(…)` alone and answers an `Error` naming the byte offset for a sheet that does not parse. Pure botopink, both targets, no host cell. (2) **`styled`**, the base for building CSS components: its own repository, `botopink/styled` at `repository/styled`, importing `css` and std. `styled "…"` — its `pub default fn`, a template function over `comptime css: @Expr<string>` — answers `@Component<Styled>` (354; alias `StyledView`; `Styled(className, rules) implement @Renderable`, the pattern of 276); `styledProperty "…"` holds declarations only (a `{`, `&` or `@` is an error at the character) and answers the record `StyledProperty`, not a component (381). Values go in `${…}` holes; Tailwind v4's CSS syntax works where it is CSS — `--spacing()`, `--alpha()`, `--theme()`, `@variant`, `@custom-variant` —; `@apply` is a `${p}` hole of a `StyledProperty` (its declarations inlined, no class of its own; 381); `@utility` is refused (a `val` or a function names a component, its parameters typed) and so is `@theme`. A component's class is std's `contentHash` over its rules, with the layer's prefix (`s_` by default; emilia's layer keeps `e_`, `contracts.md` § 4, 367); `styled` holds the layered sheet. What is computed at build and what at render is 355 / 377's. `behavior Styleable { fn toStyled(self: Self) -> StyledView; }` is `styled`'s, implemented by `StyledView`. **The theme mechanism is `styled`'s** — 300's `Theme`, `entry`, `clear`, `clearNs`, `clearAll`, `extendTheme`, the app's one `#[theme]` (358) and the cleared-breakpoint refusal —, read in a literal with `@variant md { … }` and `--theme(--breakpoint-md)`, resolved at build. (3) **emilia**, a series of components built with `styled`, run at compile time only (369): its families answer the record `StyledProperty` (381; `fn padAll(n: i32) -> StyledProperty { return styledProperty "padding: --spacing(${n});"; }`), its variants wrap them in a `styled` (`styled "&:hover { ${inner} }"`), its tokens reach a tag through `#[emilia(…)]` (369, 382, 383), and `defaultTheme()` holds Tailwind's values; its own sheet model goes (368); it imports `styled` and std, no framework (113). (4) **None of the three knows `.bpp`**, the manifest, `data-s` or jhonstart. **jhonstart** (its core, 361) holds the whole integration: the style function, found by `bpp`'s `#[style]` (361; a template function over the section's text answering a scoped `@Component<Styled>`); the scope id (module path and section line, sanitised to `[a-z0-9-]`); run-time holes as CSS variables — the rule reads `var(--s-<n>)`, the template's root element gets `style="--s-<n>: …"`, escaped —, which replaces `#[defineVars]`; the root context `StyledContext` it provides (354, 352); the sink that writes the render's one sheet (emilia's layers, then scoped styles in render order) in the head and each boundary fill; and `#[styled(comptime decl: @Decl, comptime ..items: @Expr<Styleable[]>)]` (364), taking the application's `styled` components only (369: `<button #[styled(btn)] #[emilia(.Pad.All.4)]>`). `jhonstart-emilia` and `jhonstart-styled` are deleted. (5) **A component activates a scoped style with `use`**: `use styled """.title { font-size: 2rem; }""";` in its body, or `use cardStyle;` for a style several components share; `html` reads it in the function's hooks (277) and writes `data-s="<id>"` on every element that function's template writes — a child's own template is not scoped by its parent. (6) **The manifest:** `"bpp": "jhonstart"` (361). (7) **The `.bpp` format** (212): the header starts on the first line, with no opening `---`, and ends at the first separator line — `---` (the markup follows) or `--- style ---` (the style follows, closed by a `---` line, then the markup to the end of the file); a file with no separator is markup only. One style section; an unscoped rule is written with `:global(…)`; `#[isGlobal]` and `#[defineVars]` go. Errors at the line: a first line `---`; a `--- style ---` after the markup; a second style section; an unclosed one; a style section in a project whose `"bpp"` package declares no `#[bpp.style]` function (361). The section unfolds to `use <style> """…""";` in the function's body, after the header's statements, before `return`.
